Turn 1

Context attached by the editor or browser (1)
<browser_instruction># Claude in Chrome browser automation

You have access to browser automation tools (mcp__claude-in-chrome__*) for
interacting with web pages in Chrome. Follow these guidelines for effective
browser automation.

## Loading deferred tools

If the mcp__claude-in-chrome__* tools are deferred (must be loaded via
ToolSearch before use), load every tool you expect to need in ONE ToolSearch
call — the select query accepts a comma-separated list — never one call per
tool. Start with the core set:

ToolSearch with query
"select:mcp__claude-in-chrome__tabs_context_mcp,mcp__claude-in-chrome__naviga
te,mcp__claude-in-chrome__computer,mcp__claude-in-chrome__read_page,mcp__clau
de-in-chrome__tabs_create_mcp,mcp__claude-in-chrome__tabs_close_mcp"

Add task-specific tools to the same call when the task obviously needs them:
read_console_messages / read_network_requests for debugging, form_input for
forms, gif_creator for recordings, javascript_tool for page scripting.

## GIF recording

When performing multi-step browser interactions that the user may want to
review or share, use mcp__claude-in-chrome__gif_creator to record them.

You must ALWAYS:
* Capture extra frames before and after taking actions to ensure smooth
playback
* Name the file meaningfully to help the user identify it later (e.g.,
"login_process.gif")

## Console log debugging

You can use mcp__claude-in-chrome__read_console_messages to read console
output. Console output may be verbose. If you are looking for specific log
entries, use the 'pattern' parameter with a regex-compatible pattern. This
filters results efficiently and avoids overwhelming output. For example, use
pattern: "[MyApp]" to filter for application-specific logs rather than
reading all console output.

## Alerts and dialogs

IMPORTANT: Do not trigger JavaScript alerts, confirms, prompts, or browser
modal dialogs through your actions. These browser dialogs block all further
browser events and will prevent the extension from receiving any subsequent
commands. Instead, when possible, use console.log for debugging and then use
the mcp__claude-in-chrome__read_console_messages tool to read those log
messages. If a page has dialog-triggering elements:
1. Avoid clicking buttons or links that may trigger alerts (e.g., "Delete"
buttons with confirmation dialogs)
2. If you must interact with such elements, warn the user first that this may
interrupt the session
3. Use mcp__claude-in-chrome__javascript_tool to check for and dismiss any
existing dialogs before proceeding

If you accidentally trigger a dialog and lose responsiveness, inform the user
they need to manually dismiss it in the browser.

## Avoid rabbit holes and loops

When using browser automation tools, stay focused on the specific task. If
you encounter any of the following, stop and ask the user for guidance:
- Unexpected complexity or tangential browser exploration
- Browser tool calls failing or returning errors after 2-3 attempts
- No response from the browser extension
- Page elements not responding to clicks or input
- Pages not loading or timing out
- Unable to complete the browser task despite multiple approaches

Explain what you attempted, what went wrong, and ask how the user would like
to proceed. Do not keep retrying the same failing browser action or explore
unrelated pages without checking in first.

## Tab context and session startup

IMPORTANT: At the start of each browser automation session, call
mcp__claude-in-chrome__tabs_context_mcp first to get information about the
user's current browser tabs. Use this context to understand what the user
might want to work with before creating new tabs.

Never reuse tab IDs from a previous/other session. Follow these guidelines:
1. Only reuse an existing tab if the user explicitly asks to work with it
2. Otherwise, create a new tab with mcp__claude-in-chrome__tabs_create_mcp
3. If a tool returns an error indicating the tab doesn't exist or is invalid,
call tabs_context_mcp to get fresh tab IDs
4. When a tab is closed by the user or a navigation error occurs, call
tabs_context_mcp to see what tabs are available</browser_instruction>
You
We will build AutoPlanner using spec-driven development. Do not write any code yet. Only create SPEC.md, nothing else. Include: goal, users, constraints, inputs/outputs, requirements in EARS format (WHEN ... THE SYSTEM SHALL ...), pass/fail acceptance criteria (AC-1, AC-2, ...), explicit list of output files, non-goals, open questions, and a changelog table. Product: AutoPlanner, a free web app that plans a student's week automatically. - Inputs: fixed events (classes, labs), tasks (estimated hours + deadline + type), preferences (no work after a set hour, preferred focus hours, max block length, blocked times). - A scheduling optimizer (Google OR-Tools CP-SAT) places task blocks so every deadline is met, demanding tasks go in focus hours, big tasks are split into chunks, and preferences are respected. The week is shown as a calendar. - Marking a block "not done" reschedules the remaining work instantly. If the deadlines cannot all be met, the app says so clearly and suggests options (which deadline to extend or how many hours to cut) instead of failing. - It learns the user's actual time vs estimate per task type and corrects future estimates. - Export the plan to Google Calendar as an .ics file. - Adding tasks: (1) screenshot import of the MS Teams Assignments list using the Gemini vision API, with Tesseract OCR as fallback; results always shown in an editable review table; relative dates like "Due tomorrow 11:59 PM" resolved from the date of the import; (2) quick paste of assignment text; (3) manual entry. - Microsoft Teams / Graph import is out of scope: tested, and IISc requires admin consent for assignment permissions. - Demo mode with realistic sample data, so anyone can try it without setup. - Measurable results: compare the optimizer against a simple greedy earliest-deadline-first baseline on generated workloads (deadlines met, total lateness, preference violations) and report solve time. Recommended stack (list alternatives as open questions only if you see a real problem): Python 3.13, Streamlit app deployed on Streamlit Community Cloud (free), Supabase (free Postgres) for storage, OR-Tools CP-SAT, Gemini API for screenshots, pytesseract (Tesseract installed via packages.txt) as fallback, a calendar component for Streamlit. No login: each user gets a private planner link with a random ID; secrets only in Streamlit secrets, never in git. Timezone Asia/Kolkata. Time slots of 30 minutes. Planning horizon up to 14 days. The whole project must be buildable in 3 days.

I've written SPEC.md and nothing else: no code, and nothing committed. It has every section you asked for. Requirements are numbered R1–R62 so commits can refer to them, and each is tagged P0 (must ship in the 3 days) or P1 (cut first if time runs short). There are 27 pass/fail acceptance criteria (AC-1 to AC-27), and a day-by-day build order is at the end of §9. Design decisions you should check: - Dates are worked out locally, not by Gemini. Gemini only copies the due text as it appears (for example "Due tomorrow at 11:59 PM"). A local resolver turns it into a date using the import date. Gemini and Tesseract share one parser, and dates can be tested with fixed cases (AC-14 uses Wed 2026-10-14). - When the deadlines can't all be met, the solver tries three things in turn: a strict plan, then a model that finds the fewest hours that don't fit, then an "extend deadline" check for each task. The app only shows an option after re-solving with it applied and confirming it works (R30). If the solver runs out of time, the app shows the greedy plan with a banner. - Plan checking: a separate validator checks every plan before it is saved, and the benchmark uses the same check. - Benchmark: 90 seeded workloads (3 sizes × 3 load levels × 10 seeds). The greedy baseline and the scoring rules are defined exactly in R55–R56, so the comparison can't drift. - Learning formula: factor = 1 + n/(n+2)·(r−1), kept between 0.5 and 2.5. The exact numbers are tested in AC-13. Real problems I flagged (§8): - OQ-3 (blocking): AC-17 (screenshot import accuracy) needs you to supply at least 3 redacted Teams screenshots with the correct answers labelled. - OQ-4: on Gemini's free tier, Google may use what you send to improve its products. Screenshots contain course names and possibly student names, so the spec requires a notice before upload. - OQ-5: Supabase pauses a free project after about 7 days without activity, which breaks every planner link until it's restored. Demo mode doesn't use Supabase, so it keeps working. - OQ-6: streamlit-calendar may not work with Python 3.13 and the current Streamlit. I'd check it in the first hour of day 1; the spec includes a fallback. Every open question has a proposed default, so none of them blocks the build except OQ-3.

        - and min block length greater than max block length. Focus win

        - with a notice.

        +lot grid to the grid (start rounded down, end rounded up) and 
        +tell the user that it did so. |                               
    123 +| R10 | Must | WHEN the user saves a task manually, THE SYSTEM
        + SHALL check that the title is not empty, the estimate is in 0
        +.5–40 h, and the deadline is in the future. It SHALL default `
        +demanding` from the type table and store the task as `open`. |
    124 +| R11 | Must | IF a task fails validation, THEN THE SYSTEM SHA
        +LL show the reason next to the field and SHALL NOT save the ta
        +sk. |                                                         
    125 +| R12 | Must | WHEN the user edits or deletes a task, event, b
        +locked time or preference, THE SYSTEM SHALL save the change an
        +d replan (R13–R22) automatically. |                           
    126 +| R13 | Must | WHEN the user saves preferences, THE SYSTEM SHA
        +LL reject two cases: day start at or after "no work at/after",
        + and min block length greater than max block length. Focus win
        +dows that fall outside the working day SHALL be clipped to it,
        + with a notice. |

    128  ### 5.3 Scheduling
    129  
    125 -- **R14 [P0]** WHEN a plan is computed, THE SYSTEM SHALL build
        - the slot grid from "now" (rounded up to the next slot, IST) t
        -o 24:00 on the last horizon day. It SHALL enforce these **hard
        - constraints**:                                               
    126 -  1. Task slots are placed only in available slots.           
    127 -  2. At most one task occupies a slot.                        
    128 -  3. Every task whose deadline is inside the horizon gets all 
        -of its remaining work in slots that end at or before its deadl
        -ine.                                                          
    129 -  4. No block is longer than the max block length.            
    130 -  5. Every block is at least min(min block length, the task's 
        -remaining work) long.                                         
    131 -  6. Task work on any calendar day does not exceed the max wor
        -k per day.                                                    
    132 -- **R15 [P0]** WHEN a task's remaining work is longer than the
        - max block length, THE SYSTEM SHALL split the task into severa
        -l blocks, each within the min and max block lengths.          
    133 -- **R16 [P0]** WHEN a plan is computed, THE SYSTEM SHALL optim
        -ise these soft goals in strict priority order (weighted so tha
        -t a higher goal always dominates a lower one):                
    134 -  1. Demanding-task slots placed inside focus windows (minimis
        -e the slots outside them).                                    
    135 -  2. Fewer blocks (minimise fragmentation).                   
    136 -  3. Earlier completion (minimise the sum of block end slots, 
        -which leaves buffer before deadlines).                        
    137 -  4. *(P1)* Stability on replan: minimise the slots that diffe
        -r from the previous plan's future blocks.                     
    138 -- **R17 [P0]** WHEN a task's deadline is after the horizon end
        -, THE SYSTEM SHALL schedule its work on a best-effort basis in
        -side the horizon. Unplaced hours of such a task SHALL NOT make
        - the plan infeasible. The task SHALL be labelled "continues be
        -yond this plan".                                              
    139 -- **R18 [P0]** THE SYSTEM SHALL run CP-SAT with a wall-clock l
        -imit (default 10 s, configurable) and a configurable number of
        - workers (default 4).                                         
    140 -- **R19 [P0]** IF CP-SAT returns neither a feasible plan nor a
        - proof of infeasibility within the limit, THEN THE SYSTEM SHAL
        -L show the greedy EDF plan (R43) instead. A banner SHALL say "
        -Showing a quick plan — the optimizer ran out of time."        
    141 -- **R20 [P0]** WHEN any plan is produced (by the optimizer, th
        -e fallback, or diagnosis), THE SYSTEM SHALL check it with an i
        -ndependent hard-constraint validator before saving or displayi
        -ng it. IF the validator finds a violation, THEN THE SYSTEM SHA
        -LL NOT save that plan. It SHALL show an error message and keep
        - the previous plan.                                           
    142 -- **R21 [P0]** WHEN a plan is ready, THE SYSTEM SHALL display 
        -it in the calendar (week and day views) with fixed events and 
        -blocked times. It SHALL be possible to move through the whole 
        -horizon.                                                      
    143 -- **R22 [P1]** WHEN a plan is ready, THE SYSTEM SHALL show the
        - plan summary table described in §4.2.

    143  ### 5.4 Feedback and replanning
    144  
    147 -- **R23 [P0]** WHEN the user marks a block that has started (s
        -tart ≤ now) as **not done**, THE SYSTEM SHALL return the block
        -'s unfinished minutes to the task's remaining work. It SHALL k
        -eep every past block and every `done` block fixed, replan from
        - the current slot, and show the new plan instantly (§4.3).    
    148 -- **R24 [P0]** WHEN the user marks a block **done**, THE SYSTE
        -M SHALL record it as done, with the actual minutes if the user
        - gives them (default: the planned length).                    
    149 -- **R25 [P0]** WHILE a past block has no feedback, THE SYSTEM 
        -SHALL count it as done when it computes remaining work, and SH
        -ALL show it with an "unconfirmed" badge (see OQ-1).           
    150 -- **R26 [P0]** WHEN a task's remaining work reaches zero, THE 
        -SYSTEM SHALL prompt "Mark complete?" with an editable actual t
        -otal (default: the sum of the done minutes). On confirmation i
        -t SHALL store the task as `completed` with the actual hours.  
    151 -- **R27 [P1]** WHEN the user marks a future block **can't do t
        -his slot**, THE SYSTEM SHALL add a one-off blocked time coveri
        -ng that block and replan.                                     
    152 -- **R28 [P1]** WHEN the user marks a block not done with "minu
        -tes completed" > 0, THE SYSTEM SHALL count those minutes as do
        -ne and return only the rest.

        +'s unfinished minutes to the task's remaining work. It SHALL k
        +eep every past block and every `done` block fixed, replan from
        + the current slot, and show the new plan instantly (§4.3). |  
    148 +| R24 | Must | WHEN the user marks a block **done**, THE SYSTE
        +M SHALL record it as done (confirmed), with the actual minutes
        + if the user gives them (default: the planned length). |      
    149 +| R25 | Must | WHILE a past block has no feedback, THE SYSTEM 
        +SHALL count it as done when it computes remaining work, show i
        +t with an "unconfirmed" badge, and exclude it from learning da
        +ta (R36). |                                                   
    150 +| R26 | Must | WHEN a task's remaining work reaches zero, THE 
        +SYSTEM SHALL prompt "Mark complete?". The prompt SHALL:<br>• l
        +ist any unconfirmed blocks of the task, with a "Confirm these 
        +as done" option;<br>• show an editable actual total (default: 
        +the sum of the confirmed done minutes).<br>On confirmation, TH
        +E SYSTEM SHALL store the task as `completed` with the actual h
        +ours. |

    154  ### 5.5 Infeasibility and overdue work
    155  
    156 -- **R29 [P0]** IF the hard model is proven infeasible, THEN TH
        -E SYSTEM SHALL show the banner "Can't fit everything before th
        -e deadlines". The banner SHALL show:                          
    157 -  - **H**, the minimum total hours that cannot be scheduled (s
        -olved exactly by a model that allows per-task shortfall and mi
        -nimises the total), and the shortfall of each task;           
    158 -  - up to 3 **"Extend deadline"** options. Each names a task a
        -nd the earliest new deadline (on the slot grid) that makes the
        - whole plan feasible when only that task's deadline moves;    
    159 -  - one **"Cut hours"** option: reduce the listed tasks by the
        - listed hours, H in total.                                    
    160 -- **R30 [P0]** WHEN the system offers an extend or cut option,
        - THE SYSTEM SHALL have re-solved the instance with that option
        - applied and confirmed that it is feasible. Unverified options
        - SHALL NOT be shown. IF no single-deadline extension within th
        -e horizon gives a feasible plan, THEN THE SYSTEM SHALL say so 
        -and offer only the cut option.                                
    161 -- **R31 [P0]** WHILE the plan is infeasible, THE SYSTEM SHALL 
        -display the best-effort plan from the minimum-shortfall model,
        - with the unscheduled hours of each task listed next to the ca
        -lendar.                                                       
    162 -- **R32 [P0]** WHEN the user clicks an option's "Apply" button
        -, THE SYSTEM SHALL update the task (new deadline or reduced es
        -timate) and replan.                                           
    163 -- **R33 [P0]** IF an open task's deadline has passed with rema
        -ining work, THEN THE SYSTEM SHALL mark the task `overdue`, lea
        -ve it out of planning, and offer "Extend deadline" or "Mark co
        -mplete".                                                      
    164 -- **R34 [P0]** IF infeasibility diagnosis together takes longe
        -r than 15 s, THEN THE SYSTEM SHALL show the options found so f
        -ar and the best-effort plan, and note that the search was cut 
        -short.                                                        
    165 -- **R35 [P0]** IF any error occurs while planning or importing
        -, THEN THE SYSTEM SHALL show a plain-language message and keep
        - the previous state. It SHALL NOT show a stack trace to the us
        -er.

    166  ### 5.6 Learning from actual time
    167  
    169 -- **R36 [P0]** WHEN a task is completed, THE SYSTEM SHALL stor
        -e its type, its raw estimate (the user's number before correct
        -ion) and its actual hours.                                    
    170 -- **R37 [P0]** THE SYSTEM SHALL compute a correction factor fo
        -r each task type from the last 10 completed tasks of that type
        - with actual hours > 0:                                       
    171 -  - *n* is the number of such tasks and *r* = Σ actual ÷ Σ raw
        - estimate;                                                    
    172 -  - factor = 1 + (n ÷ (n + 2)) × (r − 1), clamped to [0.5, 2.5
        -];                                                            
    173 -  - with *n* = 0 the factor is 1.0.                           
    174 -- **R38 [P0]** WHEN a task is created from any source and its 
        -type has factor ≠ 1.0, THE SYSTEM SHALL:                      
    175 -  - prefill the estimate as raw × factor, rounded to the neare
        -st 0.5 h (ties round up);                                     
    176 -  - show the reason, for example "You usually take 1.3× on lab
        - reports (3 tasks)";                                          
    177 -  - let the user override the value;                          
    178 -  - store both the raw and the used estimate.                 
    179 -- **R39 [P1]** THE SYSTEM SHALL show an "Estimate insights" pa
        -nel with the factor, n and r for each type.                   
    168 +| ID | Priority | Requirement |                               
    169 +|---|---|---|                                                 
    170 +| R36 | Must | WHEN a task is completed, THE SYSTEM SHALL stor
        +e:<br>• its type and its raw estimate (the user's number befor
        +e correction);<br>• its actual hours: the sum of user-confirme
        +d done minutes plus any extra time the user enters in the R26 
        +prompt;<br>• `learning_eligible = true` only if none of the bl
        +ocks counted toward its work is unconfirmed. |                
    171 +| R37 | Must | THE SYSTEM SHALL compute a correction factor fo
        +r each task type from the last 10 completed tasks of that type
        + with `learning_eligible = true` and actual hours > 0:<br>• *n
        +* is the number of such tasks and *r* = Σ actual ÷ Σ raw estim
        +ate;<br>• factor = 1 + (n ÷ (n + 2)) × (r − 1), clamped to [0.
        +5, 2.5];<br>• with *n* = 0 the factor is 1.0. |               
    172 +| R38 | Must | WHEN a task is created from any source and its 
        +type has factor ≠ 1.0, THE SYSTEM SHALL:<br>• prefill the esti
        +mate as raw × factor, rounded to the nearest 0.5 h (ties round
        + up);<br>• show the reason, for example "You usually take 1.3×
        + on lab reports (3 tasks)";<br>• let the user override the val
        +ue;<br>• store both the raw and the used estimate.<br>Tasks th
        +at are already open are not re-estimated. |                   
    173 +| R39 | Should | THE SYSTEM SHALL show an "Estimate insights" 
        +panel with the factor, n and r for each type. |

    175  ### 5.7 Adding tasks: screenshot, paste, manual
    176  
    183 -- **R40 [P0]** WHEN the user uploads Teams Assignments screens
        -hots, THE SYSTEM SHALL send each image to Gemini with a fixed 
        -JSON response schema. Each row has: `title`, `course`, `due_te
        -xt` (copied exactly as shown, for example "Due tomorrow at 11:
        -59 PM") and `status` (`upcoming` \| `past_due` \| `completed` 
        -\| `unknown`). Gemini SHALL NOT be asked to work out dates. Da
        -te resolution is done locally (R43).                          
    184 -- **R41 [P0]** IF the Gemini key is missing, or the call fails
        -, times out (20 s), returns invalid JSON, or returns zero rows
        -, THEN THE SYSTEM SHALL run Tesseract OCR on the image and par
        -se the text with the quick-paste parser (R46). It SHALL label 
        -the result "Read with Tesseract (fallback)".                  
    185 -- **R42 [P0]** WHEN screenshot import runs, THE SYSTEM SHALL l
        -abel which engine produced each row. Before the first upload, 
        -it SHALL show the notice "Screenshots are sent to Google Gemin
        -i for reading and are not stored by AutoPlanner". Images SHALL
        - NOT be written to disk or to the database.                   
    186 -- **R43 [P0]** WHEN a due text is resolved, THE SYSTEM SHALL u
        -se the import date (default today IST, editable as "Screenshot
        - taken on") as the reference and apply these rules:           
    187 -  - `today` / `tomorrow` / `yesterday` map to that day relativ
        -e to the import date.                                         
    188 -  - A weekday name maps to the next date with that weekday str
        -ictly after the import date (see OQ-2).                       
    189 -  - A month and day without a year (`Oct 20`, `20 October`, `M
        -on, Oct 20`) maps to the year that puts the date closest to th
        -e import date.                                                
    190 -  - Explicit years are kept.                                  
    191 -  - 12-hour and 24-hour times are accepted. A missing time mea
        -ns 23:59.                                                     
    192 -  - All results are in IST.                                   
    193 -  - Text that cannot be resolved gives an empty deadline, flag
        -ged for the user.                                             
    194 -- **R44 [P0]** WHEN extraction finishes (screenshot or paste),
        - THE SYSTEM SHALL show an editable review table with columns: 
        -include, title, course, type (inferred from keywords), estimat
        -e (type default × learning factor), deadline, demanding, sourc
        -e text, and engine. The SYSTEM SHALL NOT save anything until t
        -he user clicks "Add selected tasks".                          
    195 -- **R45 [P0]** WHEN the review table is built, THE SYSTEM SHAL
        -L untick rows with status `completed`, rows that duplicate an 
        -existing task (same normalised title and course, with deadline
        -s within 1 h of each other), and rows with a past deadline. It
        - SHALL require a deadline on every ticked row before it saves.
    196 -- **R46 [P0]** WHEN the user submits pasted text, THE SYSTEM S
        -HALL parse it with a deterministic rule-based parser and show 
        -the same review table. The parser accepts the Teams list copy-
        -paste layout (title line, course line, due line) and also sing
        -le-line `title — due text` entries.                           
    197 -- **R47 [P1]** THE SYSTEM SHALL allow at most 20 Gemini calls 
        -per planner per IST day and 10 per browser session in demo mod
        -e. Beyond the limit it SHALL use Tesseract and say so.        
    198 -- **R48 [P1]** WHILE in demo mode, THE SYSTEM SHALL offer a "T
        -ry with a sample screenshot" button that runs the import pipel
        -ine on a bundled, synthetic screenshot.

    204  ### 5.10 Benchmark
    205  
    222 -- **R54 [P0]** THE SYSTEM SHALL include a seeded workload gene
        -rator. Its parameters are size (10 tasks/7 days, 20/7, 30/14) 
        -and load ratio ρ ∈ {0.6, 0.9, 1.15}. Its output:              
    223 -  - a weekday class and lab timetable, 0–3 blocked times, and 
        -default preferences;                                          
    224 -  - task estimates scaled to hit the target ρ (±0.05), with to
        -tal work ≤ 90 % of the available slots in the horizon, so that
        - all work fits somewhere.                                     
    225 -                                                              
    226 -  "Now" is fixed at Monday 08:00 of a fixed week.             
    227 -- **R55 [P0]** THE SYSTEM SHALL include a **greedy EDF baselin
        -e**, defined as follows:                                      
    228 -  1. Visit the available slots in time order, skipping slots w
        -here the day's cap is reached.                                
    229 -  2. In each slot, assign the open task with the earliest dead
        -line (ties: continue the current task, then larger remaining w
        -ork, then ID). Skip tasks whose current run has reached the ma
        -x block length.                                               
    230 -  3. Work may continue past a deadline, which counts as latene
        -ss.                                                           
    231 -  4. The baseline ignores focus windows and the min block leng
        -th.                                                           
    232 -- **R56 [P0]** THE SYSTEM SHALL compute these metrics with one
        - shared module for both methods:                              
    233 -  - **deadlines met**: tasks whose work is all scheduled at or
        - before the deadline;                                         
    234 -  - **total lateness**: Σ max(0, finish − deadline), in hours;
    235 -  - **preference violations**: demanding slots outside focus w
        -indows, plus blocks shorter than the min block length (not cou
        -nting a task's only block when its total work is below the min
        -);                                                            
    236 -  - **solve time**: wall clock in seconds, including model bui
        -lding.                                                        
    237 -                                                              
    238 -  In the benchmark, the optimizer runs in strict mode. When st
        -rict mode is proven infeasible, the optimizer switches to a mi
        -nimum-total-lateness mode.                                    
    239 -- **R57 [P0]** WHEN `python -m bench.run_benchmark --seeds 10`
        - runs, THE SYSTEM SHALL write `bench/results/results.csv` (one
        - row per instance × method), `bench/results/REPORT.md` and `be
        -nch/results/comparison.png`. The report SHALL contain:        
    240 -  - aggregate tables by size and ρ;                           
    241 -  - solve-time median, p95 and max, and the % proven optimal; 
    242 -  - environment details: CPU, Python, OR-Tools version, time l
        -imit, workers.                                                
    243 -- **R58 [P1]** THE SYSTEM SHALL show the latest `REPORT.md` an
        -d chart on a "Results" page in the app.

I've updated SPEC.md to v0.2. It's still the only file in the repo, and it isn't committed. A raw diff is mostly noise because every requirement moved into a table, so here is what changed in substance. Priority column - Requirements and acceptance criteria are now tables with a Must / Should column. Must and Should replace the old P0 / P1 labels. - There are 53 Must and 11 Should requirements, and 29 Must and 6 Should acceptance criteria. Should work starts only after every Must criterion passes. - To keep Must realistic for 3 days, two items moved to Should: - R50, exporting fixed events in the .ics file. - Plan stability on replan, which I split out of R16 into a new requirement, R64. - I added Should criteria AC-30 to AC-35, so every Should requirement has a check. - The build order now ends with a "Leftover time" phase that lists the Should items in order. Your answers, now in a new §8 Decisions (D-1 to D-10) Decision: D-1: unconfirmed blocks count as done but don't feed learning What changed in the spec: R25 and R36: a task is used for learning only if none of its blocks are unconfirmed. R26: the mark-complete prompt lets you confirm those blocks. AC-13 adds two cases: an unconfirmed task leaves the factor unchanged, and confirming it changes the factor.
────────────────────────────────────────
Decision: D-2: "Due Wednesday" on a Wednesday means next week What changed in the spec: R43 tags each resolved date as inferred, ambiguous or unresolved. R44 adds a "Date check" column (ⓘ / ⚠ / ✖) and lists rows that need attention first. AC-14 and AC-15 now check these flags.
────────────────────────────────────────
Decision: D-3: screenshots are collected on day 1 What changed in the spec: Day 1 now includes collecting your 3 screenshots and writing labels.json with you. AC-17 is based on those 3 screenshots.
────────────────────────────────────────
Decision: D-4: screenshots are never stored What changed in the spec: C9 and R42: images stay in memory only, never go to disk, the database or logs, and are dropped once the review table is built. R40 sends images to Gemini inline rather than through the Files API, which keeps uploads for 48 h. New AC-29 tests this.
────────────────────────────────────────
Decision: D-5: GitHub Action keep-alive What changed in the spec: New R63 and AC-28: a workflow pings Supabase every 3 days, using GitHub encrypted secrets, and never prints secrets or response bodies. The workflow file is added to the output files list.
────────────────────────────────────────
Decision: D-10: unguessable planner IDs What changed in the spec: IDs are now 256-bit (43 characters, up from 128-bit) and come from a secure random generator. They are checked against a strict pattern and never logged. Malformed and unknown IDs get the same "not found" message (R2, R4, R5, AC-1, AC-2). D-6 to D-9 keep the defaults you accepted. I also added changelog row 0.2. Other fixes: v0.1 pointed R19's greedy fallback at R43, which is the date resolver; it now points to R55. I also added "re-estimating open tasks" to the non-goals, per D-9. One new open question (OQ-11): will the GitHub repo be public? It matters for two reasons: - GitHub turns off scheduled workflows in a public repo after 60 days with no activity, which would quietly stop the Supabase keep-alive. - Your fixture screenshots would be published. My proposed default is to assume public: the README explains how to turn the workflow back on, and you check each screenshot before it's committed.

Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)
I've written PLAN.md and created no other files. All 38 acceptance criteria are covered and all 67 requirements are assigned to at least one task; I checked both with a script. How it's organised: there are 10 phases, and every task is tagged with its R# and AC-#. Phases 1–9 hold only Must work, about 32 hours across the 3 days; Phase 10 holds the Should items and starts only after every Must AC passes. A table maps each AC to the task that finally satisfies it, how it's checked, and a status box to tick off. Phase 1 (deployment check, about 2 hours): - The first commit is .gitignore, so private/ is excluded before any real screenshot exists. - A public GitHub repo with secret-scanning push protection is deployed to Streamlit Cloud on Python 3.13. - A temporary check page has four parts: - Environment: versions plus a tiny OR-Tools solve. - Calendar: the calendar component with a click test. - Supabase button: writes, reads and deletes one planners row, never showing the ID. - Gemini button: one call with a small image and a JSON schema. - I added the Python 3.13, OR-Tools, Tesseract and calendar checks because they are the most likely ways the deploy can break, and they cost a few minutes here instead of a lost day later. - Results go into a log table at the end of PLAN.md. Risks: the plan lists 14, each with a mitigation and the phase where it should be settled. Two are easy to miss: Windows needs the tzdata package for IST conversions, and Supabase's newer key format may not work with the Python client. Spec questions to decide (each has a proposed default in §5): - Q-1 is a real contradiction. R14.5 makes the minimum block length a hard rule, but the greedy baseline ignores it. So AC-5 and AC-11, which require greedy plans to pass the validator, would fail by design. The lateness and best-effort plans also break deadlines on purpose. I propose giving the validator separate rule sets for each kind of plan. - Q-3: ten requirements have no AC (R6, R10–R13, R21, R24, R28, R34, R35), so nothing formally passes or fails them yet. - Q-4: the spec itself says files outside its output list need a changelog entry first, and PLAN.md isn't on that list. Approving this would add one SPEC.md changelog row. - Q-6: the spec doesn't say where scheduling work for tasks due after the horizon ranks against the focus-hours goal. If it ranks lower, a demanding task due after the horizon would get almost no time. If you agree with the defaults for Q-1 to Q-4, I can fold them into SPEC.md as v0.4.

You
Decisions on the plan's spec questions: - Q-1: Accept. The validator has a base rule set every plan must satisfy (no overlaps, no blocked hours, no clash with fixed events) plus plan-type rule sets: the optimizer must satisfy all hard rules; greedy-baseline and best-effort plans have deadline misses and short blocks counted and reported, not treated as validation failures. Update AC-5 and AC-11 accordingly. - Q-3: Add a concise pass/fail AC for every Must requirement among R6, R10–R13, R21, R24, R28, R34, R35. Update SPEC.md to v0.4 (decisions + changelog), then update PLAN.md to match. Do not create any other files.     319 +| D-12 (plan Q-1) | The greedy baseline ignores the min block 
        +length, and best-effort plans miss deadlines by design. How do
        +es the validator treat them? | There is a **base rule set** th
        +at every plan must satisfy: no overlaps, no blocked or out-of-
        +hours time, and no clash with fixed events. On top of it, each
        + plan type has its own rules:<br>• optimizer plans must satisf
        +y all the hard rules;<br>• greedy-baseline and best-effort pla
        +ns also keep the max block length and the daily cap, and their
        + deadline misses and short blocks are counted and reported, no
        +t treated as failures. | R19, R20, AC-5, AC-11 |

        +focus hours. | R14.3, R17, AC-27 |

AC-27 now tests a 10 h task due in 14 days, which gets exactly 5 h in a 7-day plan. - New acceptance criteria (D-13): - Must AC-40 to AC-47 cover R6, R10–R13, R21, R24, R34 and R35. - Should AC-39 covers R28. - Every requirement now has an acceptance criterion: 47 in total, 40 Must and 7 Should. - Other defaults accepted: the daily cap counts work already done today (R14.6). Calls I made that you may want to check: - Greedy and best-effort plans still must respect the max block length and the daily cap. You only listed deadline misses and short blocks as reported-only, and both kinds of plan keep the other two limits by design anyway. - None of the nine requirements moved to Should. Each one protects security, input checking, the core plan-and-replan loop, or the public app. R34 matters most: without its 15-second cap, finding fixes for an overloaded week could take about 80 s on Streamlit Cloud. - Tasks due after the horizon get exactly their share, no more. That meets "at least" and keeps the solver model simpler. Extra hours could be placed later as a lower-priority goal if you want. Both commit messages leave out the Co-Authored-By line because your git-commit skill says never to add one. That overrides the default attribution.

Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)
Blocking all sockets breaks asyncio on Windows (needed for Streamlit's AppTest), so I'll use --allow-hosts=127.0.0.1 instead, which still satisfies the R60 "no network" guarantee while allowing the keep-alive request with just the apikey header. Now I'll move on to the engine code, starting with config.py and models.py.

Ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)
Found it: combining all three weighted terms makes the solver choke, even though each works fine alone. I'll switch to true lexicographic optimization—minimizing one goal at a time, fixing it, then moving to the next using the prior solution as a hint—matching R16's priority rule without huge weights.

    69 +class _ScrubFilter(logging.Filter):                            
    70 +    def filter(self, record: logging.LogRecord) -> bool:       
    71 +        message = record.getMessage()                          
    72 +        if record.exc_info:                                    
    73 +            message += "\n" + "".join(traceback.format_exceptio
       +n(*record.exc_info))                                           
    74 +            record.exc_info = None                             
    75 +            record.exc_text = None                             
    76 +        record.msg, record.args = scrub(message), ()           
    77 +        return True                                            
    78 +                                                               
    79 +                                                               
    80 +def install_log_scrubber(logger_name: str = "autoplanner") -> l
       +ogging.Logger:                                                 
    81 +    logger = logging.getLogger(logger_name)                    
    82 +    if not any(isinstance(f, _ScrubFilter) for f in logger.filt
       +ers):                                                          
    83 +        logger.addFilter(_ScrubFilter())                       
    84 +    return logger

Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)
Found the root cause: the CP-SAT solver's starting hint (R55's greedy plan) violates the min-block rule, causing the bad lateness and slow solve times in the overloaded cases. I'll add a separate hint generator that's EDF-based but respects block-length and daily-cap constraints, while leaving the R55 baseline untouched as the comparison/fallback.

    40          run = run + 1 if job.task_id == current else 1
    41          current = job.task_id
    42      return assignment

    45 +def hint_plan(problem: Problem) -> dict[str, list[int]]:       
    46 +    """A starting point for CP-SAT (not the R55 baseline): earl
       +iest deadline first, but placed in                             
    47 +    whole runs that respect the min and max block length and th
       +e daily cap. Work may run past a                               
    48 +    deadline. The R55 greedy plan ignores the min block, so CP-
       +SAT had to repair it and often                                 
    49 +    started far from a good plan."""                           
    50 +    grid, jobs = problem.grid, problem.jobs                    
    51 +    remaining = {job.task_id: job.required for job in jobs}    
    52 +    assignment: dict[str, list[int]] = {job.task_id: [] for job
       + in jobs}                                                      
    53 +    used: dict = defaultdict(int)                              
    54 +    last_task, last_end = None, -1                             
    55 +    s = 0                                                      
    56 +    while s < grid.n:                                          
    57 +        day = grid.day_of[s]                                   
    58 +        if not grid.available[s] or used[day] >= problem.day_ca
       +p.get(day, 0):                                                 
    59 +            s += 1                                             
    60 +            continue                                           
    61 +                                                               
    62 +        def free_run(limit: int) -> int:                       
    63 +            length = 0                                         
    64 +            while (length < limit and s + length < grid.n and g
       +rid.available[s + length]                                      
    65 +                   and grid.day_of[s + length] == day          
    66 +                   and used[day] + length < problem.day_cap.get
       +(day, 0)):                                                     
    67 +                length += 1                                    
    68 +            return length                                      
    69 +                                                               
    70 +        placed = False                                         
    71 +        for job in sorted((j for j in jobs if remaining[j.task_
       +id] > 0),                                                      
    72 +                          key=lambda j: (j.deadline, -remaining
       +[j.task_id], j.task_id)):                                      
    73 +            if job.task_id == last_task and s == last_end:     
    74 +                continue  # a run straight after the same task'
       +s run would exceed the max block                               
    75 +            left = remaining[job.task_id]                      
    76 +            want = min(problem.max_block, left)                
    77 +            if 0 < left - want < job.min_block and left - job.m
       +in_block >= job.min_block:                                     
    78 +                want = left - job.min_block  # leave a full-len
       +gth last block                                                 
    79 +            length = free_run(want)                            
    80 +            if length >= min(job.min_block, left):             
    81 +                assignment[job.task_id].extend(range(s, s + len
       +gth))                                                          
    82 +                remaining[job.task_id] -= length               
    83 +                used[day] += length                            
    84 +                last_task, last_end = job.task_id, s + length  
    85 +                s += length                                    
    86 +                placed = True                                  
    87 +                break                                          
    88 +        if not placed:                                         
    89 +            s += 1                                             
    90 +    return assignment

Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)
Monitor "benchmark completion or failure" stream ended
Most checks pass, but AC-22's lateness check fails (1280.8h vs greedy's 1165.0h) because the optimizer treats minimum block length as a hard rule in lateness mode while greedy ignores it, costing it flexibility. Per D-12, I'll make the minimum block a soft goal in lateness mode and seed the solver with whichever starting plan is less late, so it never underperforms greedy—keeping strict mode unchanged.

    347 +def cant_do(state: PlannerState, now: datetime, block_id: str)
        + -> PlannerState:                                             
    348 +    """R27: a future block can't happen: block that time (one-
        +off) and plan around it."""                                   
    349 +    block = state.block(block_id)                             
    350 +    if block is None or block.start <= now:

        +lock'}", kind="blocked", start_time=start.time(),             
    355 +                 end_time=end.time(), on_date=start.date())   
    356 +    return replace(state, events=state.events + [busy],       
    357 +                   blocks=[replace(b, status="skipped") if b.i
        +d == block_id else b for b in state.blocks])

Benchmark results on this machine, 90 workloads: 
┌───────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────┬──────────────┐
│            Measure            │         Optimizer         │    Greedy    │
├───────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────┼──────────────┤
│ Deadlines met                 │ 1725                      │ 1674         │
├───────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────┼──────────────┤
│ Total lateness                │ 782 h                     │ 1165 h       │
├───────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────┼──────────────┤
│ Preference violations         │ 3089                      │ 4479         │
├───────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────┼──────────────┤
│ Solve time, 20 tasks / 7 days │ median 1.51 s, p95 4.72 s │ under 0.01 s │
└───────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────┴──────────────┘
One trade-off to know about: on the largest overloaded weeks (30 tasks, load 1.15) the optimizer has more preference violations than greedy (1644 vs 875). It accepts those to cut lateness by about a third and meet 14 more deadlines. The README says this. Spec changes (each committed separately as docs(spec)) - v0.5, AC-3: the secret scan now looks only for real key formats. - v0.6, R16: the planning goals are solved one after another instead of as one weighted sum, which couldn't be proven within 10 s. - v0.7, D-21: each planning goal gets a time limit once a plan exists. The "hours that don't fit" figure stays exact. The solver also starts from a plan that already follows the block-length rules. - v0.7, D-22: the benchmark's workload measure now counts capacity with the daily work limit applied. - v0.8, D-23: in the benchmark's lateness comparison, the minimum block length is a preference rather than a rule, as D-12 already said for best-effort plans. Without this, AC-22's lateness check failed. No AC target or scope changed. Problems I hit and fixed - OR-Tools crash: one solver setting (repair_hint) crashed the whole process when used with several workers. I removed it; on Streamlit Cloud it would have killed the app. - Gemini model: gemini-3.8-flash returned 503 for every image request, so GEMINI_MODEL is gemini-3.5-flash-lite. On the real API it read 15/15 synthetic rows correctly. - Tesseract: it isn't installed on your PC and can't be without an admin password, so its accuracy test runs only in CI. What you need to test by hand 1. Push and check CI (AC-38, AC-36): run git push origin main. In the Actions tab, CI should go green, with the Tesseract tests run rather than skipped. You have access to browser automation tools (mcp__claude-in-chrome__*) for
interacting with web pages in Chrome. Follow these guidelines for effective
browser automation.

## Loading deferred tools

If the mcp__claude-in-chrome__* tools are deferred (must be loaded via
ToolSearch before use), load every tool you expect to need in ONE ToolSearch
call — the select query accepts a comma-separated list — never one call per
tool. Start with the core set:

ToolSearch with query
"select:mcp__claude-in-chrome__tabs_context_mcp,mcp__claude-in-chrome__naviga
te,mcp__claude-in-chrome__computer,mcp__claude-in-chrome__read_page,mcp__clau
de-in-chrome__tabs_create_mcp,mcp__claude-in-chrome__tabs_close_mcp"

Add task-specific tools to the same call when the task obviously needs them:
read_console_messages / read_network_requests for debugging, form_input for
forms, gif_creator for recordings, javascript_tool for page scripting.

## GIF recording

When performing multi-step browser interactions that the user may want to
review or share, use mcp__claude-in-chrome__gif_creator to record them.

You must ALWAYS:
* Capture extra frames before and after taking actions to ensure smooth
playback
* Name the file meaningfully to help the user identify it later (e.g.,
"login_process.gif")

## Console log debugging

You can use mcp__claude-in-chrome__read_console_messages to read console
output. Console output may be verbose. If you are looking for specific log
entries, use the 'pattern' parameter with a regex-compatible pattern. This
filters results efficiently and avoids overwhelming output. For example, use
pattern: "[MyApp]" to filter for application-specific logs rather than
reading all console output.

## Alerts and dialogs

IMPORTANT: Do not trigger JavaScript alerts, confirms, prompts, or browser
modal dialogs through your actions. These browser dialogs block all further
browser events and will prevent the extension from receiving any subsequent
commands. Instead, when possible, use console.log for debugging and then use
the mcp__claude-in-chrome__read_console_messages tool to read those log
messages. If a page has dialog-triggering elements:
1. Avoid clicking buttons or links that may trigger alerts (e.g., "Delete"
buttons with confirmation dialogs)
2. If you must interact with such elements, warn the user first that this may
interrupt the session
3. Use mcp__claude-in-chrome__javascript_tool to check for and dismiss any
existing dialogs before proceeding

If you accidentally trigger a dialog and lose responsiveness, inform the user
they need to manually dismiss it in the browser.

## Avoid rabbit holes and loops

When using browser automation tools, stay focused on the specific task. If
you encounter any of the following, stop and ask the user for guidance:
- Unexpected complexity or tangential browser exploration
- Browser tool calls failing or returning errors after 2-3 attempts
- No response from the browser extension
- Page elements not responding to clicks or input
- Pages not loading or timing out
- Unable to complete the browser task despite multiple approaches

Explain what you attempted, what went wrong, and ask how the user would like
to proceed. Do not keep retrying the same failing browser action or explore
unrelated pages without checking in first.

## Tab context and session startup

IMPORTANT: At the start of each browser automation session, call
mcp__claude-in-chrome__tabs_context_mcp first to get information about the
user's current browser tabs. Use this context to understand what the user
might want to work with before creating new tabs.

Never reuse tab IDs from a previous/other session. Follow these guidelines:
1. Only reuse an existing tab if the user explicitly asks to work with it
2. Otherwise, create a new tab with mcp__claude-in-chrome__tabs_create_mcp
3. If a tool returns an error indicating the tab doesn't exist or is invalid,
call tabs_context_mcp to get fresh tab IDs
4. When a tab is closed by the user or a navigation error occurs, call
tabs_context_mcp to see what tabs are available</browser_instruction>

The demo and calendar changes are committed and tested, so you can push now (git push origin main). Why you saw no started blocks: they existed, but the week view opened on today, so yesterday and the day before were hidden behind the "‹" button. Only three of those five past blocks lacked feedback, too. What changed: - The week view opens two days back, so recent blocks are visible straight away. "Next" still reaches the end of the plan. - More blocks to try: the demo has six past blocks over the previous two days. Five have no feedback yet, and one is already done as an example. They sit in early mornings and evenings, so they never clash with classes, the gym or dinner. The block list covers everything from midnight two days ago. - Clicking a block: - a toast confirms it ("📌 Selected: …"); - the page scrolls smoothly to the block's details; - the details sit in a bordered panel headed "📌 Selected block". A started block offers Done with "Time taken (minutes)", or Not done, and either one replans at once. Each click is applied only once, so later reruns don't jump back. - Spec v0.10: R21, R51 and AC-44 describe this. Tests: the full suite passes with plain pytest, as CI runs it: 160 passed, 1 skipped (Tesseract). The new tests check that: - the past blocks exist at three times of day without clashes; - the opening view shows at least 5 unconfirmed blocks; - a click selects once and requests the scroll; - in the real app, selecting a block shows the panel and toast, and Done with 45 minutes saves 45 minutes and updates the plan. On the live app after the redeploy: - The demo is built once per browser session, so click Reset demo (or open a fresh tab) to get the new data. - Click a block from yesterday and check that the page scrolls to the highlighted panel. Then try Done with a time and Not done five times, which is the live AC-9 check. - The scroll uses a small script running inside the page, which I couldn't test without a browser. If it doesn't scroll, the toast and highlighted panel still show; tell me and I'll adjust it.

I'll start by checking what the installed Streamlit (1.66) offers for custom components, and whether an existing paste component is usable.
Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)
The streamlit-paste-button package relies on the Clipboard API with permission prompts and inconsistent browser support, so it's not ideal. I'll check Streamlit 1.66's newer custom-components v2 API instead, which supports inline HTML/JS without a build step.
Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)
The v2 API fits: inline HTML and JS, with setTriggerValue sending a one-shot value back to Python. I'll read the rest of the docs for the mount parameters and the JavaScript API.
Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)
Since the v2 API covers everything needed, I'll build a small custom contenteditable paste component instead of using the old package, avoiding a build step or extra dependency while supporting both native paste events and an optional clipboard-read button. The clipboard-paste feature is built, tested and committed. You can push now (git push origin main). What you get on the Import tab (Screenshot section, right below "Screenshot taken on"): - A dashed box labelled "📋 Paste your screenshot here (Ctrl+V)" with your tip text. When clicked, it shows "Ready: press Ctrl+V". - The pasted image goes through the same pipeline as an upload: Gemini, the Tesseract fallback, the review table, and the daily Gemini limit. It is never stored. - A "Paste from clipboard" button appears only where the browser supports reading images from the clipboard. Elsewhere a note says to use Ctrl+V. - Dragging an image file onto the box also works. - File upload is still there, now labelled "Or upload screenshot files". How it's built: no existing component did this reliably. streamlit-paste-button (0.1.2) needs clipboard permission and has patchy browser support. So I wrote a small one on Streamlit's own custom-component API (st.components.v2), with no build step and no new dependency, which works on our Streamlit 1.66 and Python 3.13. The box takes the image from the browser's paste event, which needs no clipboard permission in any of the four browsers. Tests (AC-48): - a pasted PNG decodes to exactly the original bytes; - 8 kinds of bad input are rejected with a plain message (wrong type, GIF, broken encoding, text labelled as PNG, over 10 MB, malformed payloads); - a pasted image goes through Gemini, or the Tesseract fallback when there's no key; - no image bytes or image data remain afterwards; - a bad paste changes nothing. The full suite passes with plain pytest: 173 passed, 1 skipped (Tesseract). The app starts with a clean log. Commits: - 11973b7 docs(spec): R68 and AC-48 (v0.11). - 08b1397 feat(import): the feature. What to test by hand on the live app (I couldn't drive a real browser here). In each of Chrome, Edge, Firefox and Brave: 1. Press Windows+Shift+S and select the Teams Assignments list. 2. On the Import tab, click the dashed box (it should say "Ready: press Ctrl+V") and press Ctrl+V. You should see "Image received: reading it…", then the review table with rows labelled Gemini. 3. Copy some plain text and press Ctrl+V in the box. It should say there's no image on the clipboard, and nothing changes. 4. Try Paste from clipboard where it appears: - Chrome, Edge and Brave ask for clipboard permission the first time. - Recent Firefox shows a small "Paste" pop-up that you click. - Older Firefox shows the "use Ctrl+V" note instead of the button. 5. Uploading a screenshot file should still work as before. Side note: Streamlit logs a warning that use_container_width is deprecated in favour of width=. It still works on 1.66; the change is a one-line edit in a few places whenever you want it.

Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)
The visual polish is committed as 0683941 and not pushed. The full test suite still passes (173 passed, 1 skipped), so behaviour is unchanged. I checked every page with headless Edge at desktop (1280 px) and phone (390 px) widths, before and after. Before → after: - Theme: Streamlit's default look → a custom theme in config.toml using only Streamlit's own theme settings, no CSS hacks: - the Inter font (from Google Fonts, falling back to the system sans-serif); - slate text and the existing blue accent; - softer borders, rounder corners, and tuned heading and number sizes. - Landing: a title, a paragraph and two buttons over an empty page → a tagline, the same two buttons, a "Free · no sign-up" line, and three feature cards. - Header: the demo banner and Reset demo were misaligned → Reset sits on the title row and the banner runs full width. - Plan tab: a flat stack → a status strip (study time planned, tasks scheduled, plan status), with the calendar, block picker and Replan in one card and the export in its own card. - Tasks, Import, Settings, Results: long undivided pages with uneven headings → each group in a bordered card with a matching heading and short caption. The benchmark report now sits under the page title instead of adding a second one. - Loading: no feedback while the solver ran → "Planning your week…" and "Updating your plan…" spinners. - Empty states: a bare "No tasks yet." or "None yet." → guidance on what to do next (empty week, no tasks, no classes, no report). - Phone: columns already stacked, but the pages were hard to scan → cards and smaller status numbers make them read cleanly at 390 px. No label named in the spec or the tests changed (a few buttons only gained an icon), so no spec change was needed. Left alone on purpose, because the fixes would change behaviour: - Upload hint: it says "200MB per file", Streamlit's default, but the app accepts 10 MB. Setting server.maxUploadSize = 10 would make the hint accurate, but oversized files would then be rejected by the upload box instead of with the app's own message. - Phone calendar: the 7-day grid is cramped at phone width. FullCalendar can only switch to day view automatically through a JavaScript callback, which the component doesn't support, so phone users need to tap day.

    492  <details class="qa"><summary><span class="qnum">30</span> How 
         is it deployed, and what does it cost?</summary><div>
    493  <p>Nothing: it uses free tiers only (SPEC constraint C1). Stre
         amlit Community Cloud deploys from <code>main</code> on Python
          3.13, with Tesseract installed through <code>packages.txt</co
         de>. Supabase's free Postgres, Gemini's free tier and GitHub A
         ctions cover the rest. Phase 1 of the plan was deploying a smo
         ke page that proved Supabase and Gemini worked on Cloud before
          any feature code.</p></div></details>
    494  <details class="qa"><summary><span class="qnum">31</span> Why 
         a keep-alive workflow?</summary><div>
    495 -<p>Free Supabase projects pause after about a week without act
        -ivity, which would break every planner link. A GitHub Action r
        -eads one row every third day. It cleans the secrets first, bec
        -ause a pasted newline once broke it, and fails clearly without
        - printing values. One caveat: GitHub disables scheduled workfl
        -ows in public repos after 60 days without activity, and the RE
        -ADME explains how to re-enable it.</p></div></details>        
    495 +<p>Free Supabase projects pause after about a week without act
        +ivity, which would break every planner link. A GitHub Action r
        +eads one row every third day. It cleans the secrets first (a s
        +tray newline or quotes in a secret once caused "Malformed inpu

        +es. One caveat: GitHub disables scheduled workflows in public

    360    <h4>3. CI failed before any test ran</h4>
    361    <p><code>ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'autoplanner'<
         /code>. Locally I'd run <code>python -m pytest</code>, which a
         dds the current folder to the import path; CI runs plain <code
         >pytest</code>. <strong>Fix:</strong> <code>pythonpath = ["."]
         </code> in <code>pyproject.toml</code>. I verified it the way 
         CI runs: a fresh clone and a fresh virtual environment with on
         ly the requirements: 154 passed.</p>
